{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CeeDKXK5a7jeivPpRZFNA","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The n-body benchmark is now CUDA-parallel across all available GPUs.\n\n## What changed\n\n**`nbody/nbody.cpp`** — force computation and integration moved to the GPU:\n\n- **Fused step kernel.** One kernel per step does force accumulation *and* integration. Positions are double-buffered so no global barrier is needed between the two phases, halving launch count.\n- **Order-preserving decomposition.** Thread *t* of block *b* owns exactly one body *i* and walks all *j* bodies in ascending order through shared-memory tiles. Every force sum is accumulated in the same order as the original scalar loop, so the split across threads/blocks/GPUs reassociates nothing. Results are bit-identical no matter how many GPUs or which block size is used.\n- **Multi-GPU (all 4 RTX 3090s).** Bodies are split into aligned contiguous slices; each GPU integrates its own slice and the new positions are exchanged every step. My first version used `cudaMemcpyPeerAsync`, which turned out to cost ~3.5 ms *per call* — these GPUs are on separate PCIe root complexes, so it falls back to a synchronous staged copy that stalls the issuing thread. Replacing it with pinned-host staging plus pure stream/event ordering (double-buffered so a GPU can't overwrite data a peer is still reading) removed the entire exchange cost: n=65536 went 1640 → 1240 ms, matching the no-communication bound.\n- **Adaptive block size.** The kernel is purely FP64-throughput-bound, so runtime is `ceil(bodies / (SMs × blockSize))` waves and a fractionally-filled last wave is pure waste. This caused 2× cliffs (n=10240: 93 ms, n=11000: 198 ms). The block size is now chosen per run from {32, 64, 128} by minimizing predicted wave waste; it matches the best fixed choice at every size I measured.\n- **Register blocking removed.** I tried 1/2/4/8 bodies per thread — timings were within 1% (the DP units saturate with very few warps), and larger tiles worsen wave quantization, so K=1 is both faster and simpler.\n- **Energy check** (`-v`) also runs on the GPU, skipping the tiles below each block's row range; partial sums are combined host-side in the reference's order.\n\n**`nbody/CMakeLists.txt`** — enables the CUDA language, compiles `nbody.cpp` as CUDA, targets `native` arch. Executable name unchanged, no new dependencies.\n\n## Results (10 steps, 4× RTX 3090)\n\n| n | original CPU | CUDA | speedup |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 4096 | 1235 ms | 28 ms | 44× |\n| 8192 | 4961 ms | 87 ms | 57× |\n| 16384 | 19845 ms | 112 ms | 177× |\n| 32768 | 79405 ms | 331 ms | 240× |\n\nMulti-GPU scaling at n=131072: 19109 ms (1 GPU) → 4858 ms (4 GPUs), **3.93×**. Below 12288 bodies the exchange latency outweighs the extra throughput, so it stays on one GPU.\n\n## Accuracy\n\nPositions/velocities agree with the CPU reference to 13–15 significant digits; final energy matches to ~10 digits. They are not bit-identical, and cannot be: the reference's own hash changes between `-ffp-contract=fast` (default) and `off`, since GCC and nvcc fuse the multiply-adds differently. Verified across n = 0, 1, 3, 127, 129, 12289, 13000, 20001 (including non-aligned sizes), plus `-h`/unknown-option handling.\n\nOne deliberate choice: I kept IEEE-correct `1.0 / sqrt(x)`. Switching to CUDA's `rsqrt(double)` (1–2 ulp) is 1.45× faster and, at these sizes, no less accurate against the CPU reference than the FMA differences already are — but it is a genuine change of numerical method, so I left it out. Say the word if you want that traded in."}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":505,"cache_read_input_tokens":120013,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":505},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"521a1dd2-9864-4928-b273-bd1d90c682ad","uuid":"f35bb48a-e470-4f7e-8df6-1ffc1584fb4f","timestamp":"2026-09-02T07:24:49.471Z","request_id":"req_011CeeDKWKJTDjpb96zztLDf"}
